## Non-interference (A.U0.04)

Applied from the first executing agent on:

- One worktree per executing agent (`git worktree add`); auditors and verifiers never write in the lead's checkout.
  Implementation agents work on disjoint files only.
- Every port-binding command (`scripts/test.sh`, `npm test`, twin runs, coverage) runs under
  `flock /tmp/sensors-audit-ports.lock` until U0 proves network-namespace isolation (M.PROC.003 (5a)); firmware builds
  under `flock /tmp/sensors-audit-toolchain.lock`. Isolation proven 2026-10-06 (U0 record (5a)): from then on each
  port-binding command runs in its own network namespace (`unshare -n`, `lo` up) instead of taking the ports lock, and
  parallel twin runs each get their own worktree, since the twin keeps its state in the tree.
- Every firmware build cleans the shared `mpy-cross/build`, so builds and `setup_toolchain.py test` run under the toolchain
  lock; `test` also rebuilds the shared `build-standard` Unix port, so it runs only while no suite or twin is running (learned 2026-10-06:
  the baseline harness, started before the lock existed, built unlocked over a gate's build and broke both; both re-ran
  alone and passed). A private toolchain copy (with its own `setcap`) only when the shared
  one is locked longer than the agent can wait, never by default.
- Before a rerun, the twin's FRAM log is read; `digital_twin/fram_state.json`, `digital_twin/scd30_state.json`,
  `digital_twin_ci_logs/`, `htmlcov*/`, `coverage*.xml` and failed-run output move to `audit/archive/<UTC timestamp>/`
  (the last 3 kept). Only pure scratch is deleted; evidence is moved, never deleted.
- Fault planting and repeat runs are budgeted first, run on tmpfs, at most about 20 repeats, as confirmation only. A
  timing failure under contention stays unconfirmed until reproduced alone. A retry is never a fix (OR37.a (2)).
- Worktrees and scratch branches go when their unit closes.
- Every unit closes only after the silent-failure scan (`audit/sweeps/silent_failure_scan.md`, OR147) has run over its
  changes and swept each class it found project-wide, in every operating mode the changes take part in (boot, reset
  countdown, flash and FRAM writes, network, calibration, recovery, reconfiguration, load; OR148); the result is in the
  unit's record.

## Owner-review list (OR2.c)

Decisions taken on the owner's behalf during execution, for the B5 review.

| unit | decision | reasoning |
|---|---|---|
| U9 | SF-B2: the notification coordinator recognises the default (no-LED) sink instead of the sink changing its `False` return | keeps the sink's documented contract unchanged; the more reversible of the two options |
| U18 | SF-M2-01: an empty SSID returns to hotspot mode directly instead of counting as a failure streak | keeps the unit reachable; the owner rule's "second STA failure streak" presupposes real STA attempts; reversible |
| U18, U22 | SF-M2-02: LED alerts gated on "clock set" rather than "synced" | the clock stays accurate long after a missed sync; the LED is the unit's only output |
| U31 (U18 stage) | SF-M2-04: a station on the hotspot gets its own LED pattern (1500 ms on, 1500 ms off); the hold-bound question stays open in BACKLOG | visibility only, no behaviour change; reversible |
| U19 | SF-M4-01 (b): Microdot's exception prints are routed to the level-gated logger by rebinding `microdot.print_exception` from our module; `ext/microdot.py` stays unedited | wrapping from our own code, as the vendoring rule allows; DebugLevel 0 then keeps the console quiet |
| U11 | SF-M1-01: one SYSTEM warning when the config file is absent while FRAM history survives (reset not a config reset, codes 7 and 9), instead of every store's absent-file line being persisted | a fresh filesystem and the twin's first run stay silent; one entry per such boot |
| U16 | SF-M1-03, SF-B12: a store that cannot use FRAM logs `LOG_RAM_ONLY` in its own RAM ring; the allocator's refusal is one `FRAM_FULL` entry in FRAM's logger | no new member on the chunk protocol; covers the unallocated case |
| U11 | SF-A11: the FRAM mempause auto-unpause becomes a `ticks_ms()` deadline tested on every uptime pass; its one-shot timer, waiter task and flag go | a dropped one-shot fire can no longer leave FRAM paused; removes the failed-arm branch (SF-B4) instead of patching it |
| U11 | SF-A10: the raw reset bits are exposed undecoded as a new `/status` key `ResetBits` | the row asks only that they are stored; exposing them is the one way they reach a reader |

## Parked deltas (OR2.c, OR106.a)

Findings during execution that need a change outside the work order; each passes A-C as a delta before it is applied.

|---|---|---|
| U15 | SCD30 temperature offset truncated (`src/asy_scd30_driver.py` `int(offset * 100)`): one tick = 0.01 °C (Interface Description §1.4.7), so 4,585 of 65,536 float32 inputs write one tick low and read back 0.01 K under the PUT value. Upstream Adafruit `3eb3b52` uses `round()`. Legacy deployed driver also truncates, so this is a D.1 flag (behaviour vs field behaviour). Found by the refresh, family (g). | folded (M.SRC_SENS.050: already the end state, rounding per A.U4.05, owner OR101.a) |
| U18 | NTP accepts 44-47-byte replies (`src/asy_ntp_client.py`, no length check; `tests/test_asy_ntp_client.py` pins `_truncated(44)` as accepted). The NTP header is a fixed 48 bytes; upstream micropython-lib `9ec1830` rejects shorter replies. Receiver-side tightening. Found by the refresh, family (g). | folded (M.SRC_NET.049 (1)) |
| U18 | NTP accepts a zero transmit timestamp: raw 0 maps through the era step to 2036-02-07, inside the 2025-2100 plausibility window, so a malformed reply with stratum ≠ 0 sets the RTC to 2036. Upstream micropython-lib `5139530` rejects it. Receiver-side tightening. Found by the refresh, family (g). | folded (M.SRC_NET.049 (2)) |
| U36 | CLAUDE.md's chroot recipe runs `scripts/test.sh` without Node, while `tests_scripts/test_live_twin_ceiling_parser.py` asserts `node` on `PATH` (a hard prerequisite, A.U24.52): in a bare noble chroot the recipe as written fails 10 tests (found in U0's family (a) chroot leg, 2026-10-06; the same at the baseline, which carries the test). The recipe needs `env --tier generic` (or a pinned Node) before `test.sh`; it lands with the recipe's move and form (the recipe moves to SPEC B.17 in U36, M.SPEC.045; A.U1.05 is the bench bridge recipe). | folded (M.SPEC.045 (1)) |

| U16 | SF-B1 (first silent-failure pass, verified by lead against `asy_fram_manager.py:645-680`, `print_log.py:224-280`): FRAM chunk identity is address + CRC only; equal-size logger chunks (30 B each) shift by exactly one chunk when a firmware change adds or removes an owner ahead of them, so a module loads a neighbour's history with a valid CRC and `/status` mislabels it. M_SPEC C.7 pitfall text and A.U16.03 (3-byte rotation) assume such a chunk reads invalid. Minimal fix (OR149): seed each chunk's existing CRC (`crc_checks.py` `add`/`check` already take `init`) with a per-owner value; CRC linearity makes a foreign equal-length chunk fail deterministically (residual = Δinit·x^(8n) mod g, non-zero for Δ ≠ 0 since g has a constant term), so the existing blank path handles it with no new bytes. A test pins the seeds distinct across every chunk-owner name in the project; A.U16.03 gains an aligned-shift case; covers every chunk type. Mode: after a firmware change. Full entry: `audit/sweeps/scan_runs/20261006_first_pass_B_own_code.md`. | folded (M.SRC_CORE.091 (1); M.SRC_SENS.062 (1)) |

Fix: test the bool, warn and count. `_DefaultSignalSink` returns `False` by design: conservative choice is the coordinator knowing it holds the default sink (no semantic change to the sink); on the owner-review list. Full entry: `audit/sweeps/scan_runs/20261006_first_pass_B_own_code.md`. | folded (M.SRC_SENS.034 (part 1)) |

| U18, U20 | SF-M2-01 (verified by lead, `asy_wifi_service.py:441-443, 476-489`): with an empty SSID (factory state or reset to defaults), `_attempt_sta_connect()` sets the failure counter to the hotspot limit without any STA attempt; after the hotspot has run once, that registers as the second failure streak and deactivates WLAN until a power cycle, e.g. after a Hostname or Country PUT answered "Valid" from the hotspot page. The owner-confirmed rule (SPEC A.4: deactivation after a second STA failure streak) assumes real STA attempts. Minimal fix: the empty-SSID branch returns to hotspot mode directly instead of faking a streak; also closes an unconfigured unit going dark after one unattended hotspot window (inventory C69). | U16 | SF-M1-02 (work-order defect, verified by lead in M.SRC_SENS.063's merged text): the "otherwise" branch tests `age < 0 or age > …` when `age` can still be `None` (an undated backup, or the NTP wait ended unsynced, `_voc_init` at 0); `None < 0` raises `TypeError` in MicroPython, so the SGP40 task dies and restarts until the supervisor reboots, on every device for as long as NTP is unreachable. | U11, U19, U14, U36 | SF-M4-01 (verified by lead: no `set_exception_handler` in `src/`/`buildgen/`; `extmod/asyncio/core.py:294-300` default handler prints; `ext/microdot.py:1518` prints before our handler): at `DebugLevel` 0 the console still receives unretrieved-task tracebacks, Microdot's exception print and cyw43 warnings. With a USB host holding the port open without reading (up to 500 ms per write), a task crash can hold the loop past the watchdog, so the crash reads as a plain watchdog reset before errno 5 or the task budget persists; a persisted raised `DebugLevel` makes it a boot loop. Minimal fix: one `set_exception_handler` in `start_tasks()` routed to the level-gated logger; Microdot's print routed through its module-level name from our wrapper (no edit to `ext/`); SPEC sentences. | U19 | Owner requirement OR150 (2026-10-08), the answer to BACKLOG owner question 1 ("Bound how long a hotspot client holds the unit?"): "1: a, because the user may want to do other settings after setting the wifi, still using the same coupled device. it only must be sure that once no clients are connected to the hotspot anymore, the device will try to connect to the actual wifi settings without a reboot required." Question 2 ("Leave the hotspot after a non-credential PUT?") answered "2. a." (owner, 2026-10-08). Asked in session whether a Wi-Fi setting saved while a phone is on the hotspot should wait until it leaves, the owner kept the 5 s leave: "Answer (a), keep it. If Wifi is changed, connect to it in reasonable time. Further settings can be done within the Wifi, no need to keep it on the hotspot. Otherwise, it might be stuck with stale clients which connected. So that 5s leave and connect is the correct behavior." (owner, 2026-10-08), and accepted the window-end race ("don't worry too much about this, joining in the last seconds is critical anyway"). All match the code; the end-to-end path had only per-step tests. | folded at U19 (lead): SPEC A.4 and A.8, BACKLOG's two questions out, an end-to-end test (client joins, leaves, window, STA retried, no restart) |
| U22 | Owner requirement OR151 (2026-10-08): the second failed STA streak after a hotspot phase no longer switches Wi-Fi off. The owner: "Don't permanently switch WiFi off, only stop retrying to connect. Instead, scan the available SSIDs in a sensible period (several minutes, ideally derived or identical to one of the existing periods). The WiFi LED stays dark, even if it is activated, being completely silent in that mode. It should look like turned off." "If the SSID stored in the configuration is found in the available networks, run one reconnection try (LED visible, completely normal reconnection attempt). This repeats with the periodicity of the SSID scans if not successful, not capped" "If the SSID stored in the config never appears, everything stays silent" "Entering the hotspot mode only through a reboot, as is." `WLAN.scan()` blocks the VM up to 10 s (`extmod/network_cyw43.c:236-246`, v1.29.0, past the 8,388 ms watchdog), so the availability check is a silent connect attempt every `hotspot_time_min`, the LED dark until a link: (owner, 2026-10-08: "a, go ahead and add it to all layers and tiers affected, and add extensive unit tests, of all types (function, error handling, resilience, self-healing, non-blocking, regression) as specified in our rules. Do these additions in an appropriate, fitting phase, no need to do this immediately."). Supersedes the permanent deactivation (owner, 2026-07-13, `368fa83`; 2026-08-11, `acc4993`) and the deactivated LED pattern (owner, 2026-09-29; 2026-10-02). | folded at U22's start (A-C: no conflict with the code or an owner decision; added M.SRC_NET.223, .224, M.TSC.231, M.SPEC.163, M.DOCS.110, .111, M.TEST_UNIT.346, .347; amended M.SRC_NET.077, .081, .087, .104, M.GEN.034, M.TSC.088, M.SPEC.016, .021, .058, .064, .082, .146, M.DOCS.026, .027, .048, .060, M.HW_BENCH.071, .073, .110, .124, .130, M.TWIN.040, .058, .059, .132, .144, M.TEST_UNIT.210, .221, .296; `audit/actions/C.md` A.C.05) |
| U22 | Owner requirement OR152 (2026-10-08): a Wi-Fi settings change lands in the state a reboot gives. The owner: "As far as I remember, setting WiFi credentials landed in the same state as after a reboot: * It tries to connect * If that fails, it did not turn WiFi off, but went into hotspot mode once (the user might have set wrong credentials and would be locked out otherwise) * ...same behavior as after reboot contiues". The code (and the legacy firmware, `async_connect.py`'s reconnect) resets neither the failure streak nor the hotspot-used-once state on a settings reconnect, so new credentials that fail after a hotspot phase switch Wi-Fi off (silent mode after OR151) rather than offering the hotspot again. Told so, the owner: "Point 1: Unify it. Simplify it. Any change of credentials, no matter from which side, lands in the same mode as "initial connection attempt after a fresh boot". All further steps follow normally. This means one operation flow covering all needs." (owner, 2026-10-08). So every settings-triggered reconnect, from the hotspot page or the home network, enters the fresh-boot state (a fresh streak, the hotspot available once); the window-end reconnect keeps its meaning (one more STA streak, then OR151's silent mode). With it, the stations check returns to the legacy rule: whatever the check cannot confirm as a client counts as no client, an exception also persists its warning, and the driver's uninitialised 32-entry answer (the radio not up: `cyw43_wifi_ap_get_stas()` returns no status and leaves the count at the buffer size, `lib/cyw43-driver/src/cyw43_ctrl.c:717-730`, `extmod/network_cyw43.c:371-386`, v1.29.0) counts as a failed check; a failed radio command already reads as an empty list there. A glitch cannot close the hotspot under a client, since the next good check five seconds later stops the window again, and an unattended unit cannot be held on the hotspot. This replaces the U18 rule "a failed stations query leaves the hotspot timer as it was" (agent, 2026-10-06) (owner, 2026-10-08: "yes, record it that way", to the agent's proposal). | folded with OR151 at U22's start (M.SRC_NET.223 (fresh-boot hook), .224 (the stations rule), with their tests, twin and bench parts listed there) |
| U26 | The U22 start fold found M.HW_BENCH.071 and .073 saying "`GET /networking` must report the STA mode", while `GET /networking` returns the config schema only (`tests_hardware/bench/test_network_resilience.py:567-568`) and .071 quotes the owner: "No STA-phase field is added to /networking" (before U22; not edited by the fold) | parked: U26 settles how the bench reads the STA mode at its start, through A-C |

## Findings

| ID | title | area | severity | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| X01 | `tests/test_uart_comm_hazard.py` `_hammer_faulted` (`:1077`): the per-failure retention bound (< 16 B) failed once in family (b)'s gate at `-1` (704 B over 30 failures = 23.5 B) on a 4-core host carrying two gates, twelve twins and the baseline; the test and everything it imports are byte-identical to the baseline; 10/10 repeats under the same load pass, and the gate's `-1` row re-run alone passes (87/87). Not reproduced alone; the bound's load sensitivity goes to U30's restructure of this measurement (`M.TEST_UNIT`, `_hammer_faulted.hammer`) | tests | low | closed (U30, lane G `f221308`). A reading inside a run counts blocks held only then; the faulted hammer reads three windows after a 10-failure warm-up and takes the least (`tests/test_uart_comm_hazard.py:1116-1118`, `:1213-1214`), checked by `_check_hammering_a_faulted_link_never_raises_and_still_recovers` (`:1354`) and the planted-stall case (`:1373-1374`), at its file's GC stage |
